### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

### Page 2

PCB CCL upgrading continues: M10 is under qualification, which should start 
shipments from 2H27, while we expect M11 shipments to start no later than 2030  
For the PCB CCL industry overall, the key revenue/profit driver in our view is the solid 
specification upgrading trend (e.g., AI M7 ramp up in 2024, the M8 ramp in 2025, and 
M9 in 2026, Exhibit 3) which continues to drive high-end CCL players’ ASP growth by 
25-50% YoY annually in 2024-2026, and we are seeing the trend continuing and 
accelerating in coming years, despite the concerns of some investors in our 
conversations that a technology migration slowdown is impacting CCL players’ 
revenue/profit growth opportunity.  
During the TPCA show, we saw both EMC and TUC launch their M10/M11 solutions 
at the event, while both companies mentioned they are closely engaging with end 
customers for the next-generation AI R&D projects, based on the M10/11 solution (+ 
under qualification now). We believe EMC should start shipping its M10 products from 
2H27 at the earliest for specific AI customer’s GPU AI rack solutions and that shipments 
should ramp up significantly in 2028 as more GPU and ASIC AI projects start adopting 
this solution. For TUC’s M10 products, we believe the company will start shipment 
from 2028 for not only high-end 3.2T Ethernet switch projects but also for some ASIC 
projects.  
Both EMC and TUC are currently under verification with clients for their M10/M11 grade 
CCL, which incorporates Q-glass. In terms of the possibility of adopting PTFE-based 
CCL, the new solution continues to face production difficulties, making us less 
positive on the solution’s progress, and we only expect the MP (mass production) 
time to occur after 2H28 at the earliest (our supply chain checks suggest the 
production yield for PTFE-based PCB is running at 10-15% vs. average yield of 
80-90% for PCB with M9 grade CCL adopting Q-glass). As a result, we believe the PCB 
upgrade for switch tray in Nvidia’s Rubin Ultra GPU servers starting from 2H27 could 
have a high likelihood of adopting M9/M10 grade CCL with Q-glass/HC (Hydrocarbon) 
instead of PTFE, potentially implying better EMC/TUC share than we currently expect 
over the next 2-3 years).

New opportunity to enter the high ASP/GM ABF substrate supply chain for TW CCL 
supply chain players 
Given the relatively slow capacity investment to date from Japanese substrate material 
suppliers (ABF CCL/ T-glass/ micro thin copper foil etc.industries are mostly dominated 
by Japanese players, with more than 95% market share in the past 10+ years) while 
demand continues to trend up (we are seeing ABF substrates’ material usage doubling 
every 1-2 years, due to the expanding substrate size/ increasing substrate layer counts / 
decreasing substrate production yield rate), we started to see end customers 
qualification processes for Taiwan and mainland China ABF substrate supply chain 
vendors accelerate from 1H26, to better overcome supply constraints, implying a 
long term share gain trend from the Taiwan/mainland China new substrate material 
suppliers (including EMC and Co-Tech in our coverage space). We therefore believe 
EMC likely starts taking ABF substrate CCL market share from 2H26 (TAM could be 
up from US$0.8bn-1.2bn in 2026 to US$2.0bn-2.5bn in 2027 and US$4.0bn-4.5bn in

2028, accounting for ~15%/19%/22% of EMC’s 2026/27/28E revenue, per GSe, 
which not only suggests significant upside potential for EMC’s ASP outlook (ABF 
substrate CCL ASP per full sheet is ~US$1,500 vs. M7/8 only at US$130/180, Exhibit 
5) but should also lead to a solid GM uptrend. We estimate the ABF substrate CCL GM is 
now at 50%+ which could further increase closer to the industry average of ~70%+ 
in the long term based on our industry checks (vs. our 2026/27/28 GM estimate on 
EMC is 35.2%/40.7%/43.4%). Also, we believe that micro-thin copper foil, which is 
dominated by Japan vendors, should also start to see some new suppliers starting from 
late 2027/early 2028, while Co-Tech said that it is preparing R&D resources and is 
working on designs for the new production lines for this new substrate-level product, 
which we estimate could start contributing to the company’s revenue from 2H28 at the 
earliest per GSe.  
Moreover, considering that the ABF substrate size/layer count both continue to increase, 
with less favorable yield rate and production efficiency, we expect the high-end AI ABF 
substrates consumption on ABF CCL to increase significantly. Based on prevailing 
specifications, we believe a quarter sheet of ABF CCL can currently produce ~6-8 units 
of high-end ABF substrates, but that number could decrease significantly to 2-3 units 
per quarter sheet in 2028 based on the substrate design, which should lead to a solid 
unit volume demand uptrend.  
In terms of supply, EMC said that it could have 1.5mn sheets/month capacity by end of 
2027 for ABF substrate CCL during TPCA event, which would then likely account for 50% 
of the global ABF substrate CCL capacity on our estimates, suggesting a strong focus by 
the company to compete in this high ASP/GM product segment.

PCB/CCL upgrades to continue with limited risk from CPO adoption 
We see limited near term risk of Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) adoption as the industry 
should start with switch trays in GPU servers from 2028E, when we estimate switch 
board to account for 14% of a total CCL TAM of US$48bn in 2028E. In considering an 
extreme case where all switch boards are downgraded to M4/M7 grade CCL, we would 
expect the impact to result in a <10% reduction in the 2028E CCL TAM, an impact which 
we believe would be manageable. At the TPCA show, we saw ZDT showcase its 30 layers

HDI PCB switch board, and the company said that it is confident that switch board will

transceivers are being used as the mainstream solution for scale out, which requires high

we see this technology as unlikely to be available before 2030E given its technology

complexities.

PCB/CCL in switch trays, will remain mainstream in the foreseeable future and thus is
beneficial to the optical module mSAP suppliers (we expect optical module mSAP to see
87% 2026-28E CAGR, Exhibit 8). We believe ZDT is well-positioned to gain share in this
fast growing market as we believe the company is proactively expanding mSAP capacity

(we expect ZDT’s mSAP capacity to reach 5x in the next 2 years, see here, as well as the
company currently holds an industry-leading yield of 90%+ for its optical module mSAP
products. Optical module mSAP GMs currently average 40%-45%, and could potentially
reach 50%+ following pricing hikes and yield improvements, which would be beneficial
to ZDT’s profitability (our 2026/27/28 GM estimate on ZDT only at
24.7%/27.6%/31.4%).

### Page 7

gainers
EMC to see solid upside on profit potential with the new substrate CCL opportunity
We continue to see EMC make progress in the new ABF substrate CCL business, a market
which the company entered in early 2026 and started producing high-end ABF substrate
CCL from 2H26, with monthly revenues reaching NT$150-200mn (<1% of the company’s
Sep monthly revenue) for the most high-end TPU substrate CCL market (see here). We
believe EMC could become one of the market leaders within the next 3-5 years (we are

expecting 20-30% market share in 2028E and trending up significantly afterward), given
its track record of successfully entering new markets over the past two decades.
Furthermore, the company is well-positioned to gain share in the tight supply market as
the company is proactive with its substrate CCL expansion plans, and the company
expects to expand its substrate CCL capacity to 4-5x of current level by 2027 (current
substrate CCL capacity at 300k sheet/month), which should account for 50% of the

global substrate CCL capacity by end of 2027E, implying a good opportunity for the
company to further ramp up market share in the long term.
M10/11 upgrades still the key to earnings growth for EMC & TUC in the next 3+
years, while the favorable S/D outlook should continue to drive the like-for-like
base pricing in coming quarters
We see EMC and TUC being well-positioned in the ongoing CCL upgrade trend as both
companies are currently under verification with clients for their respective next

generation M10/M11 grade CCL and are also future proofing their product-portfolios by
continuing to develop PTFE-based CCL. For the new M10 grade CCL, we expect EMC will
start shipments from 2Q27-3Q27 while TUC should start shipments from 2028, leading
to meaningful upside potential for each company’s revenue, as EMC/TUC’s current ASPs
have been approximately US$63/46 in 2026 while the M10 grade CCL ASP is currently
priced at ~US$800 per sheet, implying substantial ASP upside potential from here, all

else equal.
We continue to see a solid S/D outlook for high-end CCLs in coming years (see here) as
capacity expansion for these CCLs continues to track slower than demand growth (see
here). However, we note TUC revised up its capacity target and expects it to increase
2.5x+ over 2026-30 (8/5/30% upward revision to the year-end target capacity for
2026/27/28 from previous guidance), and based on company comments we believe the
new capacity is fully booked by clients, implying a solid revenue/profit growth outlook.

Co-Tech is currently obtaining LTAs to secure high-end copper foil capacity,
suggesting a solid long term market share and revenue outlook
We believe Co-Tech is well-positioned to benefit from the structural demand for high
end copper foil in a shortage environment, as the company announced capacity
expansion plans for a new Plant 4 in Malaysia, with capacity being secured by more than
one client, per the company. The company now target its annual HVLP3+ effective

capacity to reach 10K/20K/30K tons by end of 2027/28/29 (equivalent of
833/1666/2500 tons/month by 2027/28/29 end vs. current HVLP3+ effective capacity
of ~250 tons/month). This should enable the company to achieve further gains in market
share amid high end copper foil supply shortage environment (see here), as well as
seeing a better pricing environment going forward.
We expect Co-Tech’s new capacity, after ramp up, should continue to see 100% UTR, as

25 October 2026   7
Goldman Sachs Taiwan TechnologyFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM

### Page 8

most of the new capacity has been booked/sponsored by end customers already, 
implying a solid market share and revenue outlook in coming years. Moreover, we believe 
the company will start to recognize customer subsidies from 2027 at the earliest, 
implying further incremental upside to profitability in coming years.  
ZDT continues to maintain key one-stop shop status for all ABF/BT/HDI/HLC/mSAP 
products, suggesting solid market share outlook in long term 
We remain positive on ZDT as the company continues to make progress with its optical 
module mSAP and AI PCB business, and the company mentioned that both of these 
business are seeing capacity bookings by clients. We believe ZDT will become a market 
share leader in the optical module mSAP industry (see here), as we expect the company 
to expand mSAP capacity by 5x over the next two years based on announced capex 
plans. Most suppliers in the optical module mSAP industry are proactively expanding 
capacity as CPO adoption risk remains limited.  
Also, considering that ZDT is the only company that manufactures all types of 
ABF/BT/HDI/HLC/mSAP products, it is well positioned for further share gains not only in 
the optical transceiver market, but also in the AI PCB, ABF, BT markets. Additionally, we 
estimate that these new products will likely carry 35-50%+ GMs in 2027, implying a solid 
revenue/profit outlook for the company going forward.  
We also see other companies within the CCL/IC substrate sector flagging supply 
tightness across the whole supply chain as industry expansion has not been sufficient to 
meet prevailing demand. This is in line with our view that suppliers with timely capacity 
expansion plans are better positioned to make further market share gains and benefit 
from stronger pricing/profitability amid a supply-constrained environment.

### Page 10

Investment Thesis - Elite Material  
EMC, a key supplier of high-end HDI material (70%+ market share) and SLP material 
(90%+ market share), has been focusing on the high-speed switch/server CCL market for 
3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$10,200 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers.

### Page 11

Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$3,010 is based on a 22x 2027E P/E (+2x 
STDV higher than the past 3-year industry average P/E multiple).  
Key downside risks:  (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL segment; 
(2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch shipments 
globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Co-Tech 
Co-Tech is a global leading general server copper foil supplier, with ~60% market share 
in 2025 per GSe. We expect the company to enjoy solid share gain opportunities in the 
near term, considering its proactive HVLP capacity expansion plan and strong 
know-how in the advanced copper foil industry. Also, adoption of HVLP4 copper foil 
from AI servers and 1.6T networking switches starting in 2H26 should be a key demand 
driver for Co-Tech, in our view. Additionally, the company’s proactive pricing strategy 
and ability to improve production yield should drive revenue momentum in the long 
term.  
We are Buy rated as we believe Co-Tech will see better revenue/profitability in the long 
term given its shift to a more premium product mix along with proactive capacity plans 
(Plant 3 to be ready by end of 2027) and this should lead to strengthened valuations.

Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$940 is based on a 22x 2028E P/E, which is 
+1SD above the company’s average upcycle valuation in the past 10-years. 
Key downside risks: (1) earlier than expected CPO adoption in the computing IC, (2) 
slower than expected yield rate improvement speed, and (3) much stronger than 
expected capacity growth from HVLP peers, limiting pricing power

### Page 12

Investment Thesis - Zhen Ding Technology
Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in
Shenzhen, China; it has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market compared
to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% ABF
substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the most

from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025-27E CAGR) due to
the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. We also
expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to drive
ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 39% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We see
further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due to
our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which

should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,025 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology,

including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 20x 2028E P/E for
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle
average valuation).
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate
ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

Idea
Yageo Corp.  | Asia Pacific
3Q preview – margin upside;
MLCC tightening thesis intact
3Q revenue exceeded guidance, and we expect OpM of 29.1%,
140bps above the Street. Robust AI and general server demand,
together with pricing, should offset consumer softness,
supporting 4Q revenue and margin guidance of “up slightly q/q.”
Stay OW; price target unchanged.
3Q preview –  better than guidance, driven by stronger volumes and pricing:
Unaudited revenue reached ~NT$49.5bn (+11% q/q, +50% y/y), stronger than

management's guidance of a "moderate increase q/q" (which we interpret as mid- to
high-single-digit growth). We did highlight in our results takeaway report (link) that
there was no pushback from management when we asked whether the guidance
was on the conservative side. We think  some conservatism was baked into the
guidance in view of uncertainties and limited visibility into the consumer segment.
As a result of the stronger top line, we now expect GM to surpass 40%, reaching

40.4% (+190bps q/q, +420bps y/y), and for OpM to increase to 29.1% (+270bps q/q,
+620bps y/y). This is still considered in line with management's  margin guidance of
up "slightly" q/q (which we interpret as 0-3% sequential growth). With the slightly
stronger top line and margin forecast, we now estimate 3Q net profit to come in at
~NT$11.6bn (+23% q/q, +82% y/y) for EPS of NT$5.63.
4Q outlook –  pricing and AI demand should continue to drive revenue growth

and margin expansion: Consumer demand is likely going to weaken in 4Q, based on
our supply chain checks, as well as PC demand coming in weaker than expected in
3Q, per IDC. However, AI and general server demand should remain robust, and
Yageo should continue to see strong momentum for non-consumer related passive
component demand.  As a result, we see management issuing 4Q revenue guidance
of "up slightly q/q" and GM/OpM guidance of "up slightly q/q".

Key issues on which we will focus in the earnings call: 1) demand visibility for non-
AI; 2) pricing actions for MLCC in 1Q, 3) pricing actions for tantalum, resistors,
magnetics, etc., 4) customer inventory levels for consumer, 5) thoughts on the TDK/
Taiyo Yuden MOU, 6) view on midrange/low-end capacity shortage in CY27.
Reiterate OW with price target at NT$1,050: This implies ~24x CY28e P/E;
however, PEG is only 0.72x. We favor Yageo for its scale, broad product portfolio

Morgan Stanley Research 7
Price target discussion and valuation methodology
Our 12-month price target is unchanged at NT$1,050:  Our price target is our base case
value, derived from our residual income valuation model. Similar to our analysis of other
tech hardware companies within our coverage, we think this methodology provides the
most accurate value of the firm by taking into account the cost of equity.
Our key assumptions are all unchanged, including:

• Cost of equity of 7.8%
° Risk-free rate of 1.0% (10-year Taiwanese government bond yield)
° Equity risk premium of 6.8%
° Beta of 1.1
• Medium-term growth rate of 12%
• Terminal growth rate of 3%
Our price target implies 32x 2027e P/E and 24x 2028e P/E. These are above its five-year
historical range of 8x to 18x, which we view as justified considering its longer-term
transformation story to become a global total solutions provider, moving from passive

into active components. In addition, compared to global passive component peers, we
believe Yageo stock remains undervalued with higher ROE and margins but lower P/E vs.
Japanese peers (which trade on an average at 30x+ P/E).
Our bull and bear case scenario values are also unchanged at NT$1,490 and NT$480,
respectively.

MS Rating
▪ There is fear that leading Japanese and

Korean MLCC suppliers are shifting more
capacity to cater to the high end, resulting in
undersupply for the midrange/low end.
▪ The same narrative that drove the 2018
cycle is now back in play.
▪ Utilization rates are increasing, so
suppliers are expecting price hikes for the
midrange/low end. Distributors and ODMs
are both building inventory.
▪ AI demand is growing and tantalum
capacitor pricing may show further price
increases in 2H.
▪ All of this should help support a re-rating

in the stock.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 126: 261027_daiwa_faraday

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_faraday.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Faraday released a mixed set of results and guidance on 27 
October. 3Q26 core results missed consensus on an unfavourable product 
mix and higher opex, while 4Q26 guidance was broadly intact on delayed 
MCU business rebalancing. Regardless of the near-term hiccup, 
management expects its 3 product lines of mass-production (MP), IP and 
non-recurrent engineering (NRE) to fire on all cylinders into 2027 for a 
double-digit % YoY revenue growth. This concurs with our turnaround story 
for the counter, and we reiterate our Buy (1) rating and 12M TP of TWD220.

What’s the impact: Results mixed, guidance intact. Faraday’s 3Q26 net 
profit came in at TWD279m, 9% above our estimate and beating 
consensus by a meaningful 38%. Despite the beat, dynamics were mixed 
(table on p.2). Core operations missed despite a higher top line, which was 
spurred by MP on stronger-than-expected MCU sales from its 50%-owned 
Artery (6907 TT, not rated) for a lower gross margin (GM) due to an 
unfavourable revenue mix as MP carries a lower GM than the other 2 lines. 
Higher opex, as a result of salary hikes and employee bonuses, further 
pressured core operations with operating profit missing our/consensus 
estimates by 36%/11%, but non-operating gains from marked-to-market 
investments more than offset the miss. Guidance calls for a roughly flat 
4Q26 top line with a 45% GM, broadly in line with our expectation.

Firing on all cylinders across all 3 lines. In our previous note, we 
expected rebalancing in the MCU business due to demand pull-in on price 
hikes and supply chain uncertainties. It failed to happen in 3Q26 thanks to 
early pull-in of OSAT capacity support, thus pushing back the rebalancing 
to 4Q26 — a key reason why top line was guided to be flat despite the 
other 2 lines of IP and NRE growing QoQ. That said, management expects 
the MCU business to return to a growth track in 2027 after the near-term 
hiccup. Its delayed advanced packaging (AP) ramp on design complexity 
and supply constraint looks set to accelerate in 2027 on OSAT capacity 
improvement which, together with a milestone of 2nm/GAA design-win for a 
cloud ASIC, should add to upside for the NRE and MP business next year. 
All in, we lower our 2026-27E EPS by 9-13% to factor in the higher opex.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we keep our 12M 
TP for Faraday unchanged at TWD220 as we maintain our 4-quarter 
forward PER target of 37x. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with key 
downside risks of silicon inflation and geopolitical overhangs leading to 
global inflation and prolonging a non-AI recovery.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 6-9% below the consensus 
forecasts likely on the street’s lower opex assumptions.

27 October 2026

Turnaround confirmed (II)

➢ 3Q26 results a mixed bag; 4Q26 guidance broadly intact 
➢ Firing on all cylinders into 2027 after near-term hiccups 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) call and 12M TP of TWD220

Company profile 
Founded in 1993, Faraday is a hybrid design service provider offering IP and ASIC design services to 
fabless, foundry and system houses. It has R&D and marketing locations in the US, Japan, China, India, 
and Vietnam.

### Page 5

5

Faraday Technology (3035 TT): 27 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 10 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Faraday’s Board of Directors (BoD) consists of 10 members, including 4 independent directors,
representing 40% of the board—significantly above the regulatory minimum of 20%. Three of the
10 directors are female, reflecting progress toward gender diversity at the board level. The roles
of Chairman and President are separated, supporting stronger governance checks and
balances. However, most non independent directors concurrently serve as executive managers,
which may elevate concerns regarding board independence and management oversight from an
ESG governance perspective.
Capital management 1
Over the past 10 years, Faraday has kept its dividend payout ratio at above 70% most of the
time, in spite of an up or down cycle. As an IC design service company without capex for
manufacturing fabs, Faraday's high dividend payout is appropriate, in our view.
Related party &
transaction 1
UMC, which is Faraday's largest shareholder, is Faraday's major foundry for its turnkey
business. For IC design service companies, a close relationship with foundries is essential since
their design should build on a foundry's process node and their turnkey businesses also require
a foundry's capacity support. UMC's support offers Faraday a competitive advantage, in our
view. Faraday has developed many standard cell and I/O libraries for UMC, covering logic and
mixed-signal specialty processes across 0.5μ to 22nm.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Faraday has set several evaluation standards for its existing and new suppliers and it requires all
suppliers to get ISO9001 and ISO14001 certifications for quality and environmental
management. In 2024, 100% of its wafer, packaging and testing providers had ISO9001 and
ISO14001 certifications. In addition, Faraday also has on-site audits (quality system and process
quality audit, CSR evaluation and environment management system) for its suppliers with a mid-
to-long term target of conducting annual onsite audits for 100% of its suppliers.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 1
As an IC design service company, Faraday outsources its manufacturing and thus has no own
production sites. As such, Faraday's water use is primarily for general operations, not for
production. Faraday continues to conduct several water reduction measures to manage water
usage.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
Faraday has established a green supply chain in accordance with the EU Directive on the
Restriction of the Use of Certain Hazardous Substances in Electrical and Electronic Equipment
(RoHS) and with the EU Chemicals Policy (REACH).
Faraday has a mid-to-long term target of carefully evaluating and selecting raw materials with
low hazard levels to comply with the Hazardous Substance-Free Product Standard. All of its
packaging materials are 100% lead-free and halogen-free.
E GHG emissions 1
With Faraday's energy saving measures, it reduced the GHG emissions intensity gradually. As
an IC design service company without onsite production, Faraday's GHG emissions are much
lower than other semiconductor manufacturing companies. Faraday's low power design also
helps reduce GHG emissions. In 2019, Faraday completed the development of its ultra-low
power consumption 22nm IP solution.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

OSAT 展望樂觀，SOIC 機台具技術優勢 
 2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT:2026 年弘塑產能擴充一倍 ，1Q26 已
經完成擴建 ，3Q26 新產能已經滿載。 外包部分 ， 目前外包比例33%，
未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外包產能受
限，同業也在搶外包產能。2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT，今年晶
圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年 OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，
SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026 差不多略增，2028
才會明顯大增，主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度。 
 2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量會很明顯放大:PLP 及 CoPoS 設備機
台單價很高，初估有機會是 CoWoS 翻倍，PLP 進度比較快，PLP
規格弘塑 310*310、510*515、600*600 都有，再大到 700 以上就不
做了，晶圓代工客戶規格是 310*310 但目前進度比較慢，還在實驗
線階段。力成 510*515 進度最快，4Q26 會拉第一批機台，2027 隨
力成購置的友達廠進度持續拉機台，2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量
會很明顯放大。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 3000 元：基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調
整，故維持買進評等 ，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97
元，目標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

懋、中芯、宏達等。

2Q26 毛 利率 39.37%， 低於預
期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
弘塑 2Q26 EPS 11.18 元，低於元富預估的 12.34 元，獲利達成率 91%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 17.27 億元，+8%QOQ，+6%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，
較 1Q26 回升 5.1 個百分點，然仍低於預估的 41%，主要是外包成本及急單增
加影響部分毛利率，預期 3Q26 毛利率持續向上改善，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元。

弘塑 3Q26 營收 22.67 億元，+31.3%QOQ，+51.8%YOY，營收達成率 98%，
略低於預期，預估 3Q26 毛利率 41%，預估 3Q26 EPS 16.47 元。

目前外包比例 33%，未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外
包產能受限 ， 同業也在搶外包產能 。2027 年擴產再 50-100%， 以租用廠房為主。

差不多略增 ，2028 才會明顯大增 ， 主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度，

維持買進，目標價 3000 元

維持買進評等，目標價 3000 元 
基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調整，OSAT 拉貨樂觀，長線 SOIC 機台放量可
期，故維持買進評等，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97 元，目
標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

耕興 (6146 TT)  Buy

耕興評價已修正，有機會受惠轉單效應，給 Buy 的投資建
議。  
投資建議 ：美國 FCC 有機率禁止，銷往美國的電子產品在中國
實驗室進行測試，耕興有望接受大量轉單，給 Buy 的投資建議。  
公司簡介： 耕興主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規
檢測服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零
組件之銷售，全球知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、
網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一： FCC 公布
1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機 ： 路透社 (Reuters) 報導 ，
FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電子產品，
是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試 ， 若表決通過 ，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利： 耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，
若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室 ， 將會有大量相關訂單轉移至台灣廠商 ，
耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。  
群益預估耕興 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 12.44 元 /13.73 元： 群
益預估耕興 2026 年 /2027 年營收為 49.35 億元 /53.89 億元，
YoY+9.80%/+9.19% ；稅後純益 12.68 億元 /13.99 億元，

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
Environmental ：與 2021 年相比， 2022 年之用電度數較 2021 年減少 0.68% 、
減紙 42%( 減碳 7.92 公噸 CO 2 )。未來規劃：華亞實驗室建置太陽能發電系
統；建置雨水或空調冷凝水回收系統；分階段換發 LED 節能燈具、省電空
調；並於馬達設備增設變頻器以節省用電。  
Social ：體恤員工與資助社團法人嘉義縣扶緣服務協會、新港文教基金會、
與新港奉天宮之金虎爺慢跑活動。國內交易量一百萬元以上的供應商中，
92% 已簽屬「供應商社會責任承諾書」並定期查核；零件部跟生產相關的原
料供應商皆已簽署「不使用衝突礦產聲明書」。  
Governance ：董事會治理制度包含「董事會議事規範」、衡量績效目標落實
之「董事績效評估辦法」、確保誠信經營之「誠信經營守則」、避免貪腐與
符合公平交易法之「誠信經營作業程序及行為指南」等。訂定「資訊安全政
策規範」並以 PDCA (Plan, Do, Check, Act) 循環方式管理。  
公司簡介：  
耕興成立於 1997 年，主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規檢測
服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零組件之銷售，全球
知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
由於耕興具備 GCF 、 PTCRB 組織的符合性檢測認證，以及加拿大與日本發
證權，只要客戶通過公司認證後即可銷往多國，可降低檢驗所需時間與成
本。  
根據 2022 年 FCC 所公布的資料，耕興在 5G/Wi-Fi 的檢測市占率已經是世
界第一，產能也是全球第一。手機 /網通賣的款式越多，對耕興越有利，單
一產品的出貨量對耕興營收無關。  
台灣無線通訊檢測相關同業包含東研信超 (6840 TT) 、穩得 (6761 TT) 和敦吉
(2459 TT) ，耕興的策略是不斷地去投入比同業多的 CAPEX ，做最先進規格
的檢測，像是毫米波、 Wi-Fi 7 的檢測，以提升營收與獲利。  
營收占比 _地區別：  
浩興為耕興的中國子公司。

資料來源：耕興法說簡報。

3 群益投顧 
AI 筆電、企業級 AP 檢測需求帶動耕興 1H26 營運成長：  
耕興表示， 1H26 獲利增長主要來自於實體 AI 終端裝置發展同時結合非
MRA(Mutual Recognition Arrangement ，相互承認協議 ) 國家地區 FCC 
PAG(Pre-Approval Guidance) 轉單效應帶動大量高階機種檢測需求。  
耕興內部統計最顯著增長的兩大產業族群來自於 AI 筆電及北美企業級 AP
檢測訂單需求增加。  
2H26 營運有望逐季成長：  
展望 2H26 同樣伴隨 PAG 轉單效應持續消化大量湧入的 AI 筆電及北美企業
級 AP 檢測訂單。檢測需求帶動下， 3Q26 獲利表現將有機會比 2Q26 強勁，
同時 4Q26 的獲利也有機會更上一層樓， 4Q26 是 2027 年高階旗艦級手機種
換機潮的檢測高峰期。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一：  
FCC 公布 1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機：  
路透社 (Reuters) 報導， FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電
子產品，是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試，若表決通過，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
FCC 主席表示， 2025 年賣到美國的電子產品，約有 82% 是在中國實驗室進
行測試，而在美國實驗室測試的比例不到 4%。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利：  
耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室，將會有大量相關訂
單轉移至台灣廠商，耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

### Page 7

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Document 277: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

16

share is below the likely dollar share of Japan’s MoF.

Document 330: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). T he effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current -account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4 -
quarter rolling basis ) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI -driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky -rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non -oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4 -quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it sho uld not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide  evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly . 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier -model training 
toward enterprise inference . Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data -center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore exte nd the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or natio nal security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI la cks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U .S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply -
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappo ints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

EARNINGS AGGREGATES
ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com;
The S&P 500 Earnings Scorecard is the most comprehensive and up to the minute compilation of aggregated earnings
growth and market intelligence covering the companies included in the S&P 500 Index.
This report combines I/B/E/S's unrivaled historical earnings database, in depth coverage of Wall Street analysts'
bottom-up corporate earnings estimates, and the analytic capabilities of the Proprietary Research group

and desk-top solutions.

Document 404: US SECTORS CALL_ Communication Services, Materials & Energy

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Communication Services, Materials & Energy.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US SECTORS CALL: Communication Services, Materials & Energy
Date:    7 hours ago
US SECTORS CALL: Communication Services, Materials & Energy

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US SECTORS CALL:
Communication Services,
Materials & Energy
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 25 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Noah Klassen / Unsplash

### Page 2

Investors spent the week worrying about an AI bubble. The S&P 500
slipped by 0.5%, led by a 6.2% drop in Communication Services (MW),
which was the worst-performing S&P 500 sector (table). Yet, Information
Technology fell just 0.3%. Energy (OW) led the winning sectors with a
1.6% gain, followed by Health Care (OW), up 1.5%.
Bubbles inflate valuation multiples. This year, multiples have contracted.
The S&P 500's forward P/E has fallen to 18.6 from 22.2 at the end of 2025

(chart). Information Technology's forward P/E is down to 19.0 from 26.5.
Stocks have gotten cheaper this year as earnings growth outpaced stock
price gains.
Here's more on the Communication Services, Materials, and Energy
sectors:

### Page 3

(1) Communication Services. Meta's Q3 report last Wednesday hit the
sector hard. Its free cash flow turned negative for the first time since its
2012 IPO, and it raised its 2026 capex guidance floor to $135 billion. Its
shares fell 7.0% and Interactive Media & Services fell 4.3% on Thursday,
closing down 7.0% for the week. Alphabet and Meta dominate the sector.
Together they account for 81.8% of its market cap and 70.0% of its
forward earnings (chart).

The sector's forward earnings is up 23.9% ytd, while its stock price index
is down 2.3% (chart). Its forward P/E has dropped to 17.2 from 22.1 at the
start of the year. That puts it below the S&P 500's 18.6.
Analysts expect earnings at Interactive Media & Services, the sector's
largest industry, to rise 72.3% this year, then fall 17.6% in 2027 (chart).
Yet they expect its revenues to grow 25.0% and 22.8% over the same two

### Page 4

years. Unrealized gains on Alphabet's stakes in private AI companies,
including Anthropic, inflated this year's earnings.
The de-rating isn't limited to AI. Netflix, which accounts for more than half
of Movies & Entertainment's market cap, reported Q3 results on Tuesday.
It cut its ad revenue outlook and guided Q4 earnings below expectations.
Its stock is now down 51% from its mid-2025 record high.
(2) Materials. Materials slipped 0.1% this week. Gold and Copper were

the sector's worst-performing industries, down 8.2% and 6.3% (chart).
Copper stocks fell along with the AI trade on Thursday.
Copper futures also fell slightly last week, ending at $6.48 per pound. The
AI selloff hit industrial metals on Thursday, but copper rebounded on
Friday. Rising inventories also weighed on the red metal last week.

### Page 5

Copper flowed back into London Metal Exchange warehouses, where
inventories rose to their highest since July. Traders spent the year
shipping copper to the US ahead of potential tariffs. The US price
premium that drove those flows has turned into a discount. Copper
remains near the top of an uptrend channel that began in 2016 (chart).
Forecasts have copper earnings rising 76.3% this year and 41.2% in
2027. The forward profit margin is up to 17.1%, the highest since 2022,

yet the forward P/E has dropped to 16.4. The selloff hit stock prices, not
earnings estimates.
Analysts expect Materials earnings to rise 40.2% this year and 11.8% in
2027. Materials accounts for just 1.9% of S&P 500 forward earnings and
only 1.6% of its market cap, the smallest share in over 30 years (chart).

### Page 6

We remain overweight. Materials offers rising earnings at a shrinking
multiple.
(3) Energy. Energy is up 55.1% ytd, the best-performing sector. Its
forward earnings has risen even faster, up 76.5% ytd to a record high
(chart). Energy still has the lowest forward P/E of the 11 sectors, at 13.8,
down from 15.7 at the start of the year.
Analysts see 2026 as the peak. They expect sector earnings to rise
101.9% this year, then fall 8.0% in 2027. Refining & Marketing shows this

most clearly. Its forward profit margin has soared to a record 9.0% (chart).
Its forward P/E is 9.0, with earnings forecast to fall 11.0% in 2027.

### Page 7

Oil & Gas Equipment & Services is the exception. Analysts expect its
earnings to fall 9.7% this year, then rise 25.2% in 2027 and 18.9% in 2028
(chart).
Consensus estimates assume the oil premium fades, and we expect it will
eventually. Renewed strikes on Iran after the midterms, which the US and
Israel reportedly have planned, would challenge the fading-premium
assumption. At only 3.7% of the S&P 500's market cap and a forward P/E
of 13.8, Energy is a cheap hedge against more geopolitical turmoil.

### Page 2

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
2
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged down slightly since
late September, slipping 0.9pts to 160.6
in the week ended October 17. Despite
this, growth in activity continues to track
a robust pace with quarterly momentum

running at close to 2%qtr and annual
growth at 6%yr.
• Some of this reflects revisions. Initial
figures on card activity can see changes
in subsequent weeks due to information
lags. While these are usually small,
the cumulative impact can sometimes
be material. In this case, revisions have
lifted the index in late September by
2.5pts and the 4wk trend growth rate at
the time from 0.2% to 0.6%. We will be
back-testing regular revisions to assess
whether there is a systematic bias that

can be adjusted for in future.
• The revised profile shows quarterly
growth accelerating steadily since
June, posting a 1.9%qtr gain for the
September quarter and tracking a
2.1%qtr pace in the latest week. This is
back near the robust pace seen in late
2024 and early 2025.
• The monthly and weekly reads show
a softer 0.2%mth rise in August was
followed by a solid 0.8%mth gain in
September that has largely carried into
the first two weeks of October (the latest

observation tracking a 0.4%mth pace).
• The detail shows gains are not just
due to higher fuel prices with strong
growth in discretionary services and
international transactions over the last
quarter and steady growth in essential
services as well. To the extent that the
weekly and monthly data is showing
some renewed squeeze from higher fuel
prices, this is showing through most
clearly in discretionary goods, which
have seen activity decline over the last

six weeks, with discretionary services
still posting positive, albeit slower
growth. Quarterly growth in non-fuel
card activity is tracking around 1.4%qtr.
• Official September quarter figures on
inflation and household spending due
in the coming weeks should confirm the
spending momentum and shed more
light on the extent to which it is due to
prices or volumes.

running at close to 2%qtr ...”

credit and debit card transactions processed by Westpac every day. The measures
are a timely guide to shifts in spending. See p 10 for a detailed explanation.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
3
• Chart 2 shows how quarterly growth
in the Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index compares to growth in nominal
consumption as reported in the national
accounts. Growth has shown a clear
acceleration since late June, lifting to
1.9%qtr in the September quarter and

running at 2.1%qtr in the latest week.
Weekly reads in early October have been
the strongest since July 2025.
• Only some of this reflects a price-
driven rise in fuel spend. The unwind of
temporary fuel excise tax cuts in August
and more recent surge in global energy
prices has seen local pump prices push
back above $2.40/litre locally, up from
$1.56/litre in late June.
• Excluding fuel, quarterly growth in card
activity has moderated, but only slightly,

from 1.9%qtr in August to 1.8% in
September with the latest week tracking
a 1.4% quarterly pace. The impact is
more pronounced in monthly terms with
a –0.4%mth decline in August, muted
+0.2% gain in September and subdued
+0.1% monthly pace in the latest week
(c.f. total: –0.4%; +0.2% and +0.1%).
• The 1.9%qtr rise in nominal terms and an
expected price rise of 1.1%qtr point to a
real, inflation-adjusted gain of 0.8%qtr,
in line with Westpac’s forecasts. ABS Q3

CPI figures are due shortly after we go to
press with the Q3 household spending
indicator due Nov 4 and GDP due Dec 2.

• Chart 4 shows quarterly growth in card
activity broken down by contributions
from major state and category groups.
• The contribution from essential goods
captures the effect of fuel price swings.
Outside of this, discretionary services
have been the main driver of growth

with solid support coming from essential
services. Discretionary goods activity
has essentially stalled flat. In quarterly
growth terms, essential goods is now
leading, running at 3.6%qtr (+0.9%qtr ex
fuel), with essential services tracking at
2.5%qtr, discretionary services running
at 2.0%qtr and discretionary goods much
weaker at –0.1%qtr (down materially in
per capita terms).
• By state, the large south eastern states
of NSW and Vic account for nearly two

thirds of the growth.
• As noted, the latest update includes
significant revisions. Data lags and
cleaning often result in shifts to historical
estimates. These are typically small but
can be have a material cumulative effect.
Chart 5 shows the impact of the latest
revision: for the last week of September, it
lifts the WCTI 2.5pts and the 4wk trend
growth rate from 0.2% to 0.6%, removing
what looked to be a slowing in momentum
at the time. We will be investigating this

issue more closely in coming weeks to see
whether there is a systematic bias that
can be adjusted for in the future.

• Our MHSI and retail card indexes 
are composite measures based on 
transactions in categories that are in 
scope for the ABS monthly household 
spending indicator (MHSI) and retail 
sales estimates (which have been based 
on the MHSI detail since the ABS retail 
survey was discontinued in June 2025). 
• After under-performing the broader 
WCTI in Q2, the MHSI index has moved 
more in line in Q3, posting growth of 
1.6%qtr with the latest week running  at 
2%qtr. The retail card index has been 
more subdued, up 1.1%qtr in Q3, growth 
running at just 0.4%qtr in the latest week.
• The ABS MHSI has been firmer than 
our index since June, mainly reflecting 
strong gains in vehicle sales which are 
not incorporated into our index. Vehicle 
sales appear to have been relatively flat in 
recent months, our estimates suggesting 
the official measure, due Nov 4, should 
show quarterly growth more in line with 
our indicator (Chart 7).
• The more granular detail on the retail 
card index shows non-food retail 
contracting materially over the last four 
weeks, consistent with the weakness 
being observed across discretionary 
goods categories.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 6
• The Westpac Card Tracker draws on
a rich dataset that sheds light on many
aspects of consumer demand. In some
cases, it can also reveal some nuances
about the way spend is captured – in
official statistics, industry measures and
the card data itself. Vehicles provide a

particularly good example of this.
• Vehicle spend has long been a
bellwether for the wider consumer cycle.
As a large discretionary expenditure, it is
more sensitive to interest rate changes,
‘wealth effects’ and sentiment shifts,
particularly around job security. Given
this, ABS measures and industry figures
on vehicle sales have delivered material
upside surprises over the last year.
Vehicle purchases recorded a remarkable
10.3%qtr surge in the June quarter to be

up 7.3%yr with industry figures showing
vehicle sales holding around these levels
in the September quarter. Even allowing
‘extenuating circumstances’ driving
strong demand for EVs, the results seem
to fly in the face of wider interest rate,
cost-of-living and sentiment narratives.
• The card tracker data allows for a deeper
dive. Note that credit and debit cards are
not often used to purchase vehicles with
account transfers or financing typically

used for the main purchase instead.
• However, cards are used for up-front
payments. This shows through in
payment sizes, which are in the hundreds
of dollars (average $750) not the tens of
thousands. As such, card activity should
still give some guide, particularly around
sales volumes.
• Chart 8 shows how transactions have
tracked and highlight another important
point. Most of the data in this space
focuses on new vehicles (for the ABS
this is because used sales are a transfer

between households that ‘cancels out’ for
the sector as a whole). However, industry
estimates show used sales are both more
common and have been tracking a weaker
profile – the card data suggests used sales
are down at least 3%yr (finer estimates
putting the figure closer to 8%yr).
• Age-groups offer another insightful lens.
Activity has been much firmer across
older age groups, particularly in the ‘new
vehicle’ segment where they are more
prominent (accounting for 42% of all

activity) and activity is up on a year ago.
This group has notably stronger balance
sheets and is low exposure to interest
rate rises. In contrast, vehicle spend has
been contracting materially across other
age-groups, particularly used car spend
by the ‘mortgage belt’ 35-54 year olds.
Vehicle-related card activity highlights diverging trends
